# Firmware symbol map (E-M10 Mark I, body fw 1.4)

Master index of **named / confirmed** runtime symbols recovered by static RE of the decoded firmware
container (`OLY_E_113_1400_0000_0000.BIN`). One place to look up "what lives at address X" and to see
coverage. Per-subsystem detail lives in the linked docs; this file is the cross-reference.

- **Runtime base = `0x7d991020`.** Block-0 code/data resolve at `fileoff + 0x7d991020` (NOT the
  container's stated load `0x40300000`; xrefs only resolve against `0x7d991020`). See
  `docs/firmware/memory-map.md`, `docs/firmware/flash-storage.md`.
- Architecture: Panasonic MN10300 / AM33. Immediates render **decimal** in our objdump build.
- Regenerate the full address→doc index with `python3 -I tools/symbol_index.py` (or
  `--once 0x7de57532` to locate one address). This curated table is the important subset.

## Memory / layout anchors
| address | symbol | role | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x7d991020` | `RUNTIME_BASE` | block-0 exec/reset base; reset entry | `memory-map.md`, `flash-storage.md` |
| `0x40300000` | `BLK0_LOAD` | container packaging load addr of block 0 (not exec base) | `container-format.md`, `blocks.md` |
| `0x41500000` | `BLK1_LOAD` | container load addr of block 1 | `blocks.md` |
| `0x42d00000` | `BLK2_LOAD` | container load addr of block 2 | `blocks.md` |
| `0x4035fb66` | `TASK_NAME_TBL` | id→name table, 145 records, 26-byte stride | `rtos-tasks.md` |
| `0x7d9a9384` | `TASK_CREATE_TBL` | task-creation descriptors, 134 records, 28-byte stride (`+0` id, `+12` fn, `+16` prio, `+20` stack) | `rtos-tasks.md` |
| `0x6000xxxx` | `FIRMUP_SCRATCH` | firmup RAM scratch (`0x60001130`, `0x6044cbc4`+) | `flash-storage.md`, `camera-fw-update.md` |
| `0xa0000000`–`0xa0fc7860` | `GLOBAL_DATA` | main SDRAM `.data`/`.bss` + global pointer cells (2447 distinct refs in block 0, dense at `0xa0a3`–`0xa0a9`); cached DRAM data segment | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0xa0a5c970` | `descriptor_gate_cell` | global pointer cell read by the ring-feature descriptor gate (`flags_offset 560`, `flag_mask 128`) | `community-dbfw-tool.md` |

## Firmware-update / flash path
| address | symbol | role | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x7de57e24` | `firmup_validate` | top of pre-flash image validator (fail-closed) | `camera-fw-update.md` |
| `0x7da8883e` | `region_table_parse` | walks ≤32-record region table; size-sum check (err `0x3321`) | `camera-fw-update.md` |
| `0x7da88820` | `rgn_hdr_get+8` | region-header accessor: `u32 @+8` (data ptr/fieldA) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7da88826` | `rgn_hdr_get+12` | region-header accessor: `u32 @+12` (payload length) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7da88838` | `rgn_hdr_get+22` | region-header accessor: `u8 @+22` (type selector) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7de58985` | `firmup_check_id` | image-id gate: id byte `== 0x45 'E'` (via `0x7da8880b`) | `camera-fw-update.md` |
| `0x7de573d1` | `usr_checksum` | header↔tail 20-byte duplicate-array check (err `0x3202`/`0x3204`); NOT a payload hash | `camera-fw-update.md`, `patching-pipeline.md` |
| `0x7de58970` | `TYPE_FIRMUP_WRITE_OK` | validation-passed → begin write | `camera-fw-update.md` |
| `0x7de57dd1` | `SUCCESS_REBOOT` | post-flash success/reboot | `camera-fw-update.md` |
| `0x7d9cc860` | `firmup_apply_dispatch` | 9-slot component-descriptor apply loop | `flash-storage.md` |
| `0x7d9ca000`–`0x7d9cd900` | `fmem_phys_leaf` | physical NAND read/erase/program (BML/STL) | `flash-storage.md` |
| `0x7d9cdaaa` | `flash_err_handler` | common write/erase error bail | `flash-storage.md` |
| `0x7e21d6dc` | `startFirmUp` | USB-link firmware-update entry | `usb-ptp.md`, `camera-fw-update.md` |

## Hottest helpers (by block-0 call count) — global registry / accessors
Ranked by direct `call` xrefs; these dominate the call graph, so naming them makes most disassembly
readable.
| address | xrefs | symbol | role | source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0x7e207498` | 12294 | `obj_get_f104` | field getter: `return *(a0+104)` (hottest single call target — `+104` is a very hot object field) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e2a23f0` | 129 | `objtbl792_get` | bounds-checked getter for a **131-entry pointer table at `obj+792`**: `return (u16)d0 < 131 ? *(u32*)(a0 + 792 + d0*4) : 0`. Owner object (passed in `a0`) holds a 131-slot array of sub-object pointers indexed by a small enum; callers fetch specific slots (e.g. 78, 80) and invoke methods on the result (`0x7e262972`, …). Initializer `0x7e2a2407` fills all 131 slots: loops idx 0..130, per slot builds an object via factory `0x7e24ff41`/`0x7e24ff2a` (keyed off global descriptor table `0x60459074`) + `0x7e23075e`, stores the ptr into the slot, then calls the new object's vtable method at `+148` (`calls (*(obj)+148)`); returns 1. **Dead end:** subsystem/semantic of the 131 entries unresolved — pure-code module (`0x7e2a2xxx`), no strings, numeric indices only. 129 xrefs | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e2a5f9f` | 75 | `objtbl792_get77` | **77-entry** instance of the per-class `obj+792` sub-object-pointer-table getter family (cf. `objtbl792_get 0x7e2a23f0`/131, `gss_objtbl792_get 0x7e2aae29`/159, `tbl792_get164 0x7e27f556`, …): `return (u16)d0 < 77 ? *(u32*)(a0 + 792 + d0*4) : 0`. Leaf, `retf`. Initializer `0x7e2a5fb4` fills all 77 slots via the shared factory `0x7e24ff2a`/`prop_code_dispatch 0x7e24ff41` (owner descriptor global **`0x60459348`**, so table base = `0x60459660` when owner is this global) + finalizer `0x7e23075e`, then invokes each new sub-object's vtable method at `+148` — byte-for-byte the same pattern as `objtbl792_get 0x7e2a23f0`'s builder, so this is a sibling class in module `0x7e2a`. **75 call sites, all in `0x7e2a`**, fetching fixed slots then calling methods on the result. **Dead end:** semantic of the 77 entries unresolved (pure-code, numeric indices, no strings) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e2aae29` | 126 | `gss_objtbl792_get` | **159-entry** sibling of `objtbl792_get 0x7e2a23f0`: `return (u16)d0 < 159 ? *(u32*)(a0 + 792 + d0*4) : 0` — same `obj+792` pointer-array convention, larger slot count (0..158). Owner object passed in `a0`; callers fetch a slot and invoke methods on it. A caller (`0x7e2aaf3e`) tags this cluster with module string **`"GSS"`** (`0x7e52768c`, logged via `0x7e0345c9`) before fetching slot 88 — so the `+792` sub-object-table family belongs to the **GSS** subsystem (the 131-entry `objtbl792_get` left unresolved; GSS is the tag, exact expansion still unknown). Neighbor fn `0x7e2aae40` does coordinate-scaling math on the fetched objects (type-ids 257/258, vtable `+4`/`+52` calls, scale globals `0x6045442c`/`30`/`2e`/`32`) — GSS looks geometry/coordinate-oriented. **Dead end:** GSS acronym expansion unconfirmed | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e278767` | 126 | `gss_objtbl792_get128` | **128-entry** sibling of `gss_objtbl792_get 0x7e2aae29` (159) and `objtbl792_get 0x7e2a23f0` (131): `return (u16)d0 < 128 ? *(u32*)(a0 + 792 + d0*4) : 0`. Third instance of the per-class `obj+792` sub-object-pointer-table getter. Owner descriptor global **`0x60455164`** (vs GSS's own); builder/worker `0x7e27877e` is **byte-identical** to the GSS worker `0x7e2aae40` — same factory `0x7e24ff41`/`0x7e24ff2a` + `0x7e22be56` fill, same coordinate-scaling math (type-ids **257/258**, vtable `+4`/`+52` calls, scale globals `0x6045442c`/`0x60454430`), so this instance belongs to the same **GSS** geometry/coordinate family. No per-instance module string here (inferred from the shared worker). 126 xrefs | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e2b7520` | 113 | `gss_objtbl792_get114` | **114-entry** instance of the per-class `obj+792` sub-object-pointer-table getter family (`gss_objtbl792_get 0x7e2aae29`/159, `objtbl792_get 0x7e2a23f0`/131, `gss_objtbl792_get128 0x7e278767`/128): `return (u16)d0 < 114 ? *(u32*)(a0 + 792 + d0*4) : 0`. Owner descriptor global **`0x6045c9a4`** (table base `obj+792` = `0x6045ccbc` when owner is this global). Builder/worker `0x7e2b7535` is the same GSS pattern — factory `0x7e24ff41`/`0x7e24ff2a`, fill helper `0x7e22be56`, type-ids **257/258** (`0x101`/`0x102`) dispatched via `calls`, identical coordinate-scaling globals `0x6045442c`/`0x6045442e`/`0x60454430`/`0x60454432`, finalizer `0x7e23075e` — so this instance belongs to the same **GSS geometry/coordinate** family. Callers fetch fixed slots (idx 1,2,3,…) then operate on the returned sub-object. No per-instance module string (inferred from shared worker + globals). 113 xrefs | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e2ba5bc` | 79 | `gss_objtbl792_get80` | **80-entry** instance of the per-class `obj+792` sub-object-pointer-table getter family (`gss_objtbl792_get 0x7e2aae29`/159, `objtbl792_get 0x7e2a23f0`/131, `gss_objtbl792_get128 0x7e278767`/128, `gss_objtbl792_get114 0x7e2b7520`/113): `return (u16)d0 < 80 ? *(u32*)(a0 + 792 + d0*4) : 0`. Its builder/worker **`0x7e2ba5d1`** is the same GSS pattern — factory `0x7e24ff2a`/`0x7e24ff41`, type-ids **257/258** (`0x101`/`0x102`) dispatched via `calls`, identical coordinate-scaling globals `0x6045442c`/`0x60454430`, owner-side global `0x6045cf18` — so this instance belongs to the same **GSS geometry/coordinate** family. 79 callers, all in `0x7e2b`. No per-instance module string (inferred from shared worker + globals) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e24837b` | 79 | `obj60454418_get36` | **field getter** — `return *(u32*)(obj+36)` — one of a dense **non-virtual accessor cluster** on a 184-byte class: `+24` get/set (`0x7e248393`/`0x7e24838d`), `+28` (`0x7e24839f`/`0x7e248399`), **`+36` get (this)**, `+40` (`0x7e248387`/`0x7e248381`), `+44` (`0x7e2483ab`/`0x7e2483a5`). The object is a **lazy singleton** at global `0x60454418`, materialized by **`0x7e24831c`**: `obj=*(0x60454418)`; if null → `heap_alloc_thunk 0x7e206a0f`(184) + ctor **`0x7e24825d`** (writes class **vtable `0x7e4493a0`** to `obj+0`) → cache at `0x60454418`. Neighbor `0x7e2483b1` is an index-dispatched (`d2`=field selector 3/4/…) read/write over these same offsets via a vtable `calls *(*(obj)+4)`. 79 callers concentrated in the GUI modules `0x7e31`/`0x7e30`/`0x7e24`. **Dead end:** class name/role unrecovered — stringless; structurally a GUI-app singleton state object (184 B, vtable `0x7e4493a0`) whose `+36` word is the hottest field read | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e206aa9` | 79 | `reactive_node_ctor` | **constructor of an OMReactive-derived container/node class** — `reactive_node_ctor(obj=a0, arg=a1)`: write class **vtable `0x7e42c640`** to `obj+0` (10-slot method table `0x7e206b0c/e03/e4f/bd5/…`), zero the `+4` flags word and `+8..+20`, construct **embedded sub-object at `+24`** via ctor `0x7e207558`, clear `+52`, construct **embedded sub-object at `+56`** via ctor `0x7e2075e1`, zero `+84..+108`, then wire the reactive child via **`set_child_ensure 0x7e206e92`**`(obj, arg)` and raise the ready bit via **`reactive_set_flag40000 0x7e206ece`**`(obj, 1)`. Part of the **OMReactive** C++ runtime / container-handle library (`0x7e205xxx`–`0x7e207xxx`; cf. `OMReactive_ctor 0x7e2053c1`, `set_child_ensure`, `reactive_set_flag40000`, `heap_alloc_thunk 0x7e206a0f`). Constructed from ~40 modules across `0x7e0a`–`0x7e1f` (79 callers) = a widely-reused reactive node type. **Dead end:** class name/role unrecovered — stringless, vtable carries no adjacent RTTI (same limitation noted across the OMReactive cluster) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e206b11` | 78 | `reactive_node_dtor` | **destructor of the OMReactive container/node class** — exact mirror of `reactive_node_ctor 0x7e206aa9` (same class **vtable `0x7e42c640`**, same embedded-member layout). `(a0 = node)`: rewrite vtable `0x7e42c640` to `node+0` (dtor-phase vtable), tear down the reactive chain — `0x7e207336(node)` (detach), and if predicate `0x7e206f1d(node)` holds: when global enable `*0x60452204 == 0`, pick `0x7e206bb6` vs `0x7e206bd9` on whether `0x7e20748c` (`*(node+12)`, has-children) is set, then if `*(node+20)!=0` **and** child ptr `*(node+100)!=0` release it via `0x7e207a95`; cleanup `0x7e2074aa(node)`; **destruct the two embedded sub-objects** at `node+56` and `node+24` via member dtor **`0x7e205040`** (mirrors the ctor's `+24`/`+56` sub-object construction); return `a0=0`. Part of the **OMReactive** C++ runtime cluster (ctor `0x7e206aa9`, `set_child_ensure 0x7e206e92`, `reactive_set_flag40000 0x7e206ece`, `reactive_node_eval 0x7e206f38`). 78 callers across `0x7e0a`–`0x7e1f`. **Dead end:** class name unrecovered — stringless, no adjacent RTTI | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e086927` | 79 | `playinfo_get_state` | **playback-info state-byte getter** — `return *(u8*)0x6075e75c`. The byte caches a **playback display mode/state**: writers `0x7e0877ce`/`0x7e08793e` advance it through transform helpers `0x7e08949d`/`0x7e0894b6` (passing the old value), and `0x7e087e4c` seeds it from **`code_lookup_6000 0x7db93fb8`(prop 670 = 0x29E)**; companion cache words `0x6075e75e`(u16) and `0x6075e6f8`(u16) are written alongside. Lives in the **playinfo (image-playback info) module** `0x7e08xxxx` — same cluster as **`playinfo_get_output_size 0x7e08684d`**, **`playinfo_get_pos_sum 0x7e086bbe`**, `api_play_get_exiftag_data 0x7db9a4ee`, `prop_state_by_code 0x7db98807` (with softfloat `__divdf3`/`__floatsidf` for aspect/scale math). Neighbor `0x7e086930` compares playback position counters `0x6044fd74`/`0x6044fd78` → direction code 0/1/2. Queried by 79 callers across playback/GUI modules (`0x7e14`–`0x7e1c`, `0x7e06`/`0x7e07`). **Dead end:** exact meaning of property 670 and the state-byte enum values not pinned (stringless) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e3276f0` | 76 | `select_disp_descriptor` | **id-keyed display-descriptor selector + dispatch** (`d0`=screen/page id, u8). Two actions: (1) dispatch `0x7e337fa3(id)` — a `switch(id)` for ids **1..11** via jump table `0x7e5dec` keyed also on mode global **`0x604601d2`** (28 refs = a live module state word); (2) linear-search a **10-entry × 12-byte table at `0x7e5c3474`** `{u32 id, ptr descA, ptr descB}` for the row whose id matches `d0`, and **cache that row pointer → global `0x6045fe78`**. The cached row is then consumed by sibling readers `0x7e3278a2`/`0x7e3278c5`/`0x7e327901`/`0x7e327937`/… (the per-screen draw/build path). Each `descA`/`descB` block is an **array of u16 codes** (e.g. id1→`{34,22,22,25,28,31}`, id2→`{22,28,25,31,34,22}`) — ordered field/item lists whose values (`0x16,0x19,0x1c,0x1f,0x22…`) overlap the **GUI-event field-type ids** (cf. `gui_evt_append_field25 0x7e216792`), i.e. per-screen field layouts. Module `0x7e32xxxx`; called by **76 sites in the `0x7e27`/`0x7e28` screen-class family** (objects built with vtables `0x7e473xxx`–`0x7e476xxx` that share base methods at `0x7e22b7xx`, the GUI/SubApp module). Anchors `0x6045fe78` = **currently-selected display descriptor**. **Dead end:** exact screen-id enum and the per-code field semantics not fully pinned (descriptors are numeric, no strings) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e7cf8ed` | 75 | `msgdisp_register_handler` | **message/event handler registration** into the `0x7e7cxxxx` dispatch-manager registry (`d0`=msgId u16, `d1`=subId/type u16, `a1`=arg/context, `a0`=handler fn-ptr). Gated on manager singleton **`0xa0938f78`** != 0 (built by ctor `0x7e7cf79a`, which spins an OlyOS object via `0x7d9c1598`). Allocates a node from a **fixed 200-slot × 20-byte pool at `0xa09389d8`** (allocator `0x7e7d02fe`: linear scan for a free slot `*(slot)==0`, mark in-use, `memcpy`-zero 16 B via RAM thunk `0x4700110c`, return `slot+4`), fills the 16-byte payload `{+0:u16 msgId, +2:u16 subId, +4:arg ptr, +8:handler fn-ptr, +12:next}`, and links it into the registry list (`0x7e7d0397` append via `+12` chain); returns the node (0 on pool-full / manager-absent). Callers pass a message id + handler, e.g. `d0=0x1001, d1=1, a1=1, a0=0x7e7d4456` (handler = a `switch(d0)` on sub-codes). **75 call sites, all in `0x7e7c`** — the module registers its full handler set at init through here. Companion lookups `0x7e7d03df`/`0x7e7d03b7` (find by key) walk the same list. **Dead end:** `0x7e7c` module's own name unrecovered (rodata relocated to RAM, absent from blk00); msgId namespace (`0x1001…`) not enumerated | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e065149` | 76 | `imageplayer_set_field928` | **CapImg_ImagePlayer member setter (field `+0x928`)** — `(a0`=ImagePlayer obj, `d0`=value): emits a file/line breadcrumb `trace_log_printf 0x7dfc01bf`(fmt `"F[%s] L[%d]"` `0x7e3b84e2`, file `"CapImg_ImagePlayer.cpp"` `0x7e3b84ee`, line `7248`), then `*(u16*)(obj+0x928) = d0`. Head of a **bank of per-field logged setters** for the **CapImg_ImagePlayer** class (module `0x7e06`/`0x7e07`, the Cap* capture-display family — same cluster as `capdpv_app_disp_view 0x7e07caaf`): each sibling sets a distinct member and logs its own source line — `0x7e06516f`→`+0x93a` (u8, line n/a), `0x7e065176`→`+0x978` (u8, L7264), `0x7e06519c`→next field (L7273), … All share the `"F[%s] L[%d]"` + `CapImg_ImagePlayer.cpp` trace pair. This specific entry (field `+0x928`) has **76 call sites**, concentrated in `0x7e06` (38) / `0x7e07` (23) with a scatter across the Play/GUI framework (`0x7e13`–`0x7e1b`). **Dead end:** semantic name of field `+0x928` and the sibling fields not pinned — the trace records only file+line, no field names; ImagePlayer struct layout (multi-KB) not mapped | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7dfd3045` | 75 | `chls_area_center_size` | **CHLS input-area quad → center+size rect** (`a0`=out struct, `a1`=input area obj). Null `a0`/`a1` → zero the 4-field output `{+0 cx, +2 cy, +4 w, +6 h}` (u16). Else extracts the area via `0x7dfd3198`, which reads a **4-corner quad** (4×(x,y) u16 at `a1+0/+4/+8/+12`) and logs `"INPUT AREA [%d,%d,%d,%d,%d,%d,%d,%d]"` (`0x7e375f9d`, via `trace_log_printf`); then computes bounding box: `w = x1−x0+1`, `h = y1−y0+1`, `cx = x0 + w/2`, `cy = y0 + h/2`, stored to the output struct. | `0x7e6e2506` | 115 | `bt_lock_release` | **Bluetooth-stack recursive-mutex RELEASE** (unlock). Serializes the on-camera **Bluetooth subsystem** (module `0x7e6cxxxx–0x7e6exxxx`; identifying strings `"BTEVENT_CONFIRM_NUMERIC_REQ"`, `"BTEVENT_DISPLAY_NUMERIC_IND"`, `"SEC_SetPin err"`). Recursive lock state: **owner task id `*(0x7cc381a0)`**, **nest count `*(0x7cc381a4)`**, backed by **OlySemaphore id `0x26`** (`*(0x7d9b03ca)`). Behavior: enter critical (`preempt_lock 0x7d9a16d7`), if `owner == self` then if `count==0` → clear owner, `resched_check 0x7d9a17c7`, **`OlySemaphoreSignal 0x7d9c16f9`(0x26)** (wake a waiter); else `count--`. RTOS service-call error tags **`OlyTaDD`** (`0x7E76E110`, enter) / **`OlyTaED`** (`0x7E76E118`, exit) via `rtos_svc_check 0x7d9c3e02`. **Acquire partner = `bt_lock_acquire 0x7e6e2478`** (row below; 101 xrefs). 115 callers across the whole BT stack (`0x7e6c/0x7e6d/0x7e6e`). **Note:** the E-M10 Mark I is Wi-Fi-only (no Bluetooth radio) — this is shared-codebase BT firmware for other OM-D bodies, present but not exercised on this body. First BT symbol mapped | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e6e2478` | 101 | `bt_lock_acquire` | **Bluetooth-stack recursive-mutex ACQUIRE** (lock) — exact mirror and acquire counterpart of `bt_lock_release 0x7e6e2506`. Same recursive state: **owner task id `*(0x7cc381a0)`**, **nest count `*(0x7cc381a4)`**, **OlySemaphore id `0x26`** (`*(0x7d9b03ca)`). Behavior: read self task id (`oly_cur_task 0x7d9a1c14` → `(4,sp)`), enter critical (`preempt_lock 0x7d9a16d7`); **if `owner == self`** → recursive re-entry: `*(0x7cc381a4)++` (bump nest count, no wait); **else** → `resched_check 0x7d9a17c7`, **`OlySemaphoreWait 0x7d9c1606`(id=0x26, timeout=-1 wait-forever)** to block until free, then `preempt_lock` again and set `owner = self` (`*(0x7cc381a0) = tid`). Always exits via `resched_check 0x7d9a17c7`. Same RTOS service-call error tags **`OlyTaDD`** (`0x7E76E110`) / **`OlyTaED`** (`0x7E76E118`) via `rtos_svc_check 0x7d9c3e02`. **Corrects the earlier "acquire sibling 0x7e6e2461" note on `bt_lock_release`:** `0x7e6e2461` (12 xrefs) is a *separate* BT lock on a different semaphore (`*(0x7d9b055a)`) + global (`*(0x60461ba8)`) via `0x7d9c106e`, not this one. 101 BT-stack callers; same Wi-Fi-only-body caveat as `bt_lock_release` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e6eaaf8` | 108 | `bt_get_le16` | **BT-stack unaligned little-endian u16 reader**: `return (u16)((u8)buf[1]<<8 \| (u8)buf[0])` (`a0`=byte ptr). Head of a **byte-stream serialization bank** used to parse/build BT (HCI/L2CAP/GAP) packet fields from unaligned buffers: siblings `0x7e6eab06` = **get_le32** (`b3<<24\|b2<<16\|b1<<8\|b0`), `0x7e6eab25` = **put_le16** (`buf[0]=v; buf[1]=v>>8`), `0x7e6eab31` = **put_le32**, `0x7e6eab4b` = **put_be16** (`buf[0]=v>>8; buf[1]=v`), `0x7e6eab57` = **put_be32**. 108 of its 108 callers are in the Bluetooth module (`0x7e6cxxxx` 16 / `0x7e6dxxxx` 83 / `0x7e6exxxx` 9), matching `bt_lock_release` above. BT is natively little-endian, so the LE accessors dominate; BE variants serialize network-order fields. Generic leaf (no strings); module identity from the exclusively-BT caller set. Same Wi-Fi-only-body caveat as `bt_lock_release` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e6e12ff` | 83 | `bt_opcount_dec_unlock` | **BT "leave" wrapper — decrement active-op counter + release lock**: `(*(s32)0x7cc2cc44)-- ; bt_lock_release 0x7e6e2506()`. The counter `0x7cc2cc44` is a **BT outstanding-operation / lock-holder count** bumped by the matching inc-only entry **`bt_opcount_inc 0x7e6e12ef`** (`(*0x7cc2cc44)++`, no lock) and wound back down here as part of every unlock. 83 callers spread across the whole Bluetooth stack (`0x7e6a`/`0x7e6c`/`0x7e6d`/`0x7e6e` + `0x7e6ac`/`0x7e6df`) — the standard enter/leave bracket around BT critical sections, paired with `bt_lock_acquire 0x7e6e2478`. **Dead end:** exact meaning tracked by `0x7cc2cc44` (nesting vs in-flight request count) not pinned — it is maintained but not read on any hot path seen. Same Wi-Fi-only-body caveat as `bt_lock_release` (shared-codebase BT, not exercised on the E-M10 Mark I) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e6eab71` | 107 | `bt_list_insert_after` | **BT-stack circular doubly-linked-list primitive bank** (head of the list library that sits right after the serialization bank above). Node layout: `+0` = prev, `+4` = next; the anchor/head is itself a node, so the ring is `anchor → first → … → anchor`. `0x7e6eab71` **insert_after**(`a0`=anchor node, `a1`=new node): `a1->prev=a0; n=a0->next; a1->next=n; n->prev=a1; a0->next=a1` (splice new after anchor). Siblings share the `retf [a2],4` tail: `0x7e6eab81` = **insert_before** (`a1->next=a0; p=a0->prev; a1->prev=p; p->next=a1; a0->prev=a1`, 28 xrefs), `0x7e6eab90` = **unlink_next**/detach (relink neighbours around the node, 46 xrefs), `0x7e6eab9d` = **pop/detach-and-clear** (unlink then zero the removed node's prev/next, 47 xrefs), `0x7e6eabaf` = **contains**(`a0`=anchor, `a1`=node) → walk `next` ring, `return 1` if `a1` found else `0` (63 xrefs). Bank ends before the object-init at `0x7e6eabc7`. All 107 callers in the Bluetooth module (`0x7e6cxxxx`–`0x7e6exxxx`; top prefixes `7e6dc`/`7e6db`/`7e6d0`/`7e6cb`), matching `bt_get_le16`/`bt_lock_release`. Generic intrusive-list leaf (no strings); module identity from the exclusively-BT caller set. Same Wi-Fi-only-body caveat — present in shared BT codebase, unused on this body. 3rd BT symbol family mapped | `symbol-map.md` |

Companions: `lens_dev_status_check 0x7daaa272` (general devidx form), `lens_get_dev 0x7daa651f`, `lens_dev_present 0x7daa38ae`. | `0x7d9c2e73` | 103 | `cpuconn_printf` | **cpu_conn module-local console printf** — `cpuconn_printf(fmt, a1..a7)`: `add -324,sp` big frame, copies **7** four-byte varargs from caller stack (`fmt` read at `(336,sp)`, args follow) into a local arg buffer, formats via **`0x7da82515`** (sprintf-family → `console_out 0x7d996e85`) and emits via **`emit 0x7d996ee2`**. Module identity from rodata: it is the **inter-CPU connection / gateway (cpu_conn) + RTOS CPU-context + bus-measurement diagnostics** block (strings `"CPUCONN0/1/2"`, `"CPUSW0/1/2"`, `"[cpu_conn]tid="`, `"[cpu_conn]id ="`, `"[cpu_conn][warning]OQRead!!"`/`"OEWait!!"`, `"[RTOS]CpuCtxErr"`, `"acrTskCpu"`, `"PER_WDT"`, `"GW_ERR_SIZEOVER!!"`, `"nosupportid:"`, `"wai_flg_err!!:"`, `"iset_flg001:".."iset_flg203:"`, and the bus-measure dump `"=== Bus Measure Infomation CH0 ===============\n\n"` @`0x7D9EF8ED` / `" Measure Cycle (us) : %d\n\n"` @`0x7D9EF940`, `"mode/dst/sender"`, `"STATUS = "`). | `0x7e2d9eb9` | 102 | `kv_lookup_u16` | **generic {u32 key, value} linear-search table lookup, u16 output** — `kv_lookup_u16(key=d0, count=d1, table=a1, out=(12,sp))`: scan `count` stride-8 entries `{u32 key @+0, value @+4}`; on first `entry.key == key`, store `entry.value` to `*out` as **u16** (`movhu`) and stop; no match → `*out` left untouched, no return value (`retf [d2,d3],8`). Lighter sibling `0x7e1e7802` removes without the force-destroy/log path. 263 xrefs | `symbol-map.md` |

RAM globals note: cell `0x6044cf18` and the firmup scratch `0x6044cbc4` share the `0x6044xxxx` page →
that page is a **shared RAM globals/BSS region** inside the `0x60000000` RAM aperture (distinct from
the `0xa0000000` cached SDRAM data segment above).

## Heap allocator (core memory management)
The `0x7e206a0f`/`...a17`/`...a1f`/`...a27` thunk cluster (~6.6k combined xrefs) wraps the heap. Core:
| address | symbol | role | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x7e208040` | `alloc` | tail-call → `heap_alloc` (size `d0` → ptr `a0`) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e20804a` | `free` | null-safe: if `a0!=0` → `heap_free`; manager at `0x60452220`, custom-free hook via vtbl `+16` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7db954ab` | `heap_alloc` | **size-class allocator**: buckets `≤124`→`0x7d996f56`, `≤2044`→`0x7d996fc6`, `≤16380`→`0x7d996f8e`, larger → further paths. Segregated-fit pools | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e206a1f` | `mem_free_thunk` | **1029 xrefs** — one-instruction tail-call to `free 0x7e20804a` (pointer in `a0`, null-safe). The standard free entry paired with the alloc thunks `0x7e206a0f`/`0x7e206a17`; `0x7e206a27` is an identical sibling. Dedicated row (was only noted inside the `0x7e206a0f` entry) |
| `0x7db954ed` | `heap_free` | matching free (pointer in `a0`): reads the **4-byte block header at `ptr−4`** holding the block's size, then dispatches by the same size-classes as `heap_alloc` (`≤124`→`0x7d996f64`, `≤2044`→`0x7d996fd4`, …) to return the block to its segregated-fit pool | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e206bfc` | 163 | `managed_obj_release` | **conditional managed-object release/teardown dispatcher** (heap-object lifecycle module, 163 xrefs). Marks the object in-teardown by `obj[+4] \|= 0x20000` (helper `0x7e207336`), then branches: if **pending-count `obj[+12] != 0`** (`0x7e20748c`) → invoke the object's own `vtable[+52]` (flush/defer), and when it reports more pending, call `vtable[+52]` again then `vtable[+4]` (deferred path — object not freed yet). Else (idle): if predicate `0x7e206f1d` (`obj[+52]!=0 \|\| global 0x604521f4!=0`) is false → run `vtable[+8](a1=obj+56)`; otherwise test `obj[+4] & 0x40000` (mask const `*0x7e42c570`) — if set and object non-null, run the virtual destructor `vtable[+0]` then a follow-up virtual call; if clear, **`operator delete 0x7e206a1f`(obj)**. Generic infrastructure (no class string anchors it); flag bits: `+4` bit17 (`0x20000`) = in-teardown, bit18 (`0x40000`) = has-custom-dtor-vs-plain-free; `+12` = outstanding-operation/refcount | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7db22d85` | 241 | `evflag_set` | **set event-flag bits and release satisfied waiters** (RTOS event-flag-group `set`, 241 xrefs). Args `a0`=flag-group object, `d0`=bit pattern. Runs under IRQ lock `irq_save 0x7d9c580c`(IPL=2)…`irq_restore`. Requires valid byte `obj[+4]==1`. ORs the new bits into the group's flag word via helper `0x7db2314a` (`or_bits8`: `*(obj+8) \|= bits`). Then walks the waiter list head `*(obj+24)`, `next` at node`+12`, testing each with predicate `0x7db231ca` (`evflag_waiter_match`): mode byte node`+1` (`1`=AND-all → `(bits & mask)==mask`; else OR-any → `(bits & mask)!=0`), mask at node`+4`. On a match it decrements the waiter's pending-count byte node`+0` (sentinel `0xFF` = special/report node → `0x7db23238`, a trace/report wrapper via logger `*0x606ce3e8` / `0x7db4c246`) and, when the count hits 0, unlinks the waiter from the list (wake). Core scheduler primitive; pairs with the wait-side (not yet labeled) that builds these waiter nodes | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7db34313` | `smallmem_alloc` | **"Small Memory" fixed-block pool alloc** (263 xrefs; counterpart to `smallmem_free 0x7db344fd`). Arg `d0` = size; size ≤ **128** uses this pool, larger → second-pool path `0x7db343aa` (base `0x606dbcb0`). Under lock (`0x7d9c580c(1)`): pops the free-list head index from `0x606cf908` (u16); if `0xFFFF` the pool is exhausted → fatal `cam_damage_record 0x7db19c09` (code `0xA163000`, line 368). Else sets head = `entry[+2]` (next-link), marks `entry[+2]=1` (allocated) and `entry[+0]=size`, returns block address `*(0x606dbcac) + index*128`. Maintains used-count `0x606cf90a`++, high-water max-count `0x606cf90e`, max-size `0x606cf90c`. Table entry at `0x606CF924 + index*4` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7da9674c` | 155 | `pcnt_pool_free` | **fixed-block memory-pool block release** for the `[MEC_PCNT]` / `PCT_COM` pool (debug-dumpable "Small Memory / Large Memory" pool; sibling model to `lens_mem_free 0x7dac2a86`). Takes block ptr `a0`, classifies by address into one of two sub-arenas and frees under an **IPL=1 critical section** (`irq_save 0x7d9c580c(1)` … `irq_restore 0x7d9c5851`): **Small arena** base `*0x606ba6a4` (buffer `0x606b9084`), span `0x1200` (4608 B), **128-byte** blocks → 36 slots, status table `0x606b8fec`, free-list head `0x606b8fd8`, count `0x606b8fda`; **Large arena** base `*0x606ba6a8` (buffer `0x606ba694`), span `0x400` (1024 B), **512-byte** blocks → 8 slots, status table `0x606b907c`, head `0x606b8fe0`, count `0x606b8fe2`. Each status slot is a u16-pair `{link@+0, state@+2}`: free requires `state==1` (busy) else error (Small `0x1500b004`, Large `0x1500b005`); on success clears the slot, pushes the block index onto the arena FIFO (old head → slot link, index → head) and decrements count. Pointer in neither arena → error `0x1500b006`, handled by `0x7da91d8d`. Pool manager struct at `*0x606b8fcc` (field getters `0x7da96570`=+52, `0x7da9657e`=+56); init `0x7da965a7` seeds both bases + tables (Small 36, Large 8). Debug dump (`0x7da96836`, via `0x7da886fb`/`0x7da93a8a`) prints `=== Small Memory ===`/`=== Large Memory ===`, `Used Block/Size/Max`, `Area Size`, per-slot `USED/---- (%d) Size=%04d Adress=0x%08x`, tagged `PCT_COM`/`[MEC_PCNT] Client [0x%02x]`. **Dead end:** what this pool actually stores (picture-count / MEC component client data) not pinned statically | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7dac2a86` | 158 | `lens_mem_free` | **lens-memory block release / recycle** (158 xrefs, callers in the `0x7da9xxxx` lens cluster). Wraps worker `0x7dac2aa2` in an IPL=3 critical section (`irq_save 0x7d9c580c(3)` … `irq_restore 0x7d9c5851`). Worker classifies the freed pointer `a0` by address into one of **two lens arenas** off base `*(0x606c4a84)`: `[base, base+0xF80]` (3968 B) = **`--lens_mem small--`** (descriptor `*0x606c4a88`), `[base+0x1000, base+0x7000]` (24576 B) = **`--lens_mem large--`** (descriptor `*0x606c4a94`); a pointer in neither range → error trace id `0x14009005` (`trace_log 0x7d997b14`). The arena descriptor is a **recycle FIFO** {read-idx `+0`, write-idx `+6`, buffer base `+8`}: `0x7dac2af4` tests full via `0x7dac2bdc` (`*(d+0)==*(d+6)` → trace `0x14009003` "queue full"), else pushes the block index via `0x7dac2bec` (`inc` write-idx `+6`, store). These arenas hold lens optical-correction data — neighbours in rodata: `depth`, `mtf_av/mtf_freq_num/mtf_freq_pos/mtf_capture/mtf_global` (lens MTF tables) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7dac29b7` | 146 | `lens_mem_alloc` | **lens-memory block acquire** — allocator counterpart of `lens_mem_free 0x7dac2a86`, same two-arena recycle FIFO. Wraps worker `0x7dac29d6` in an **IPL=3 critical section** (`irq_save 0x7d9c580c(3)` … `irq_restore 0x7d9c5851`), returns block pointer in `a0`. Worker selects the arena by requested size `d0` (u16): `0` → null; `≤128` → **small** arena (descriptor `*0x606c4a88`, pool `[*0x606c4a84, +0xF80]`); `≤8192` → **large** arena (descriptor `*0x606c4a94`, pool `[base+0x1000, +0x7000]`); `>8192` → further/error path `0x7dac2a44`. Pops a free block from the chosen arena's FIFO via `0x7dac2a5d` (empty test `0x7dac2bcd` → on empty, `trace_log 0x7d997b14` id **`0x14009002`** "queue empty" + null; else dequeue `0x7dac2c12`), then bounds-checks the popped pointer lies within that pool. Complements free's full-marker id `0x14009003`. 146 xrefs | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7dab6275` | 144 | `evtrace_record` | **binary event-ring recorder** — appends one fixed **20-byte** record to a **818-entry circular buffer**. Args: `a0`=4-byte ASCII tag ptr, `d0`/`d1`=two u32 payload words, `a1`=a third word. Gated by enable flag **`*0x606bb7a4`** (u16; 0 ⇒ no-op) and buffer base **`*0x606bb798`** (null ⇒ no-op). Under `irq_save 0x7d9c580c(1)` … `irq_restore 0x7d9c5851`: entry ptr = `base + index*20` (index **`*0x606bb79c`**), fields `+0..3`=tag bytes (copied from `a0`), `+4`=**timestamp** (`0x7d996ba7` → `0x3FFFFFFF − SFR 0x98900074`, i.e. a free-running down-counter read as an up-counting tick), `+8`=`d0`, `+12`=`d1`, `+16`=`a1`; then `index++`, wrap to 0 at **818**. Init/reset `0x7dab6076` (clears enable, re-allocs the ~16360-byte pool). Callers (lens/command cluster `0x7da9xxxx`–`0x7daaxxxx`) wrap the call in a per-channel enable test `0x7daaf41f(ch)` and pass a 4-char event tag, so this is a lightweight lens/command **binary trace log** distinct from the text `trace_log 0x7d997b14`. **Dead end:** tag string vocabulary and which subsystem owns the 818-slot channel not fully enumerated (region is the lens-comm/command path; recorder itself is generic) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7db344fd` | `smallmem_free` | **"Small Memory" fixed-block pool free** (275 xrefs; separate from the segregated-fit `heap_free` — this is the dedicated small-block pool whose report reads `"=== Small Memory ===  Used Block/…Max Num/…Area Size"`, rodata `0x7DBE9D6C`+). Validates the pointer is inside pool region `[*(0x606dbcac), +0x9600)` (38400 B = 300 × **128-byte** blocks); index = `(ptr-base)>>7`, parallel 4-byte table at `0x606CF924` (`+0` = block size, `+2` = status(`1`=allocated) / free-list next-index). On free: require status `==1` (else double-free), clear, push block index onto free-list head `0x606cf908` (entry`+2` = old head), decrement in-use count `0x606cf90a`. A second pool region is checked at `0x606dbcb0` (branch `0x7db34573`). Out-of-range or double-free → fatal **`cam_damage_record 0x7db19c09`** (code `0xA163000`, line 503/573). Counters: used `0x606cf90a`, size `0x606cf90c`, max-num `0x606cf90e` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d996fb8` | 143 | `scheap_large_free2` | **locked public free entry for the `scheap` large-block heap** — a thin per-worker thunk (`mov 0x7d9973a4,a1; call call_locked_arg 0x7d997031`) that runs the worker `0x7d9973a4` under the global heap critical section. Worker frees a pointer in the **large scheap region** (base ≈ `0xA0A53F40`, **27 blocks × 16448 B** stride `0x4040`): computes block index `(ptr − base)/0x4040`, range-checks `0..26` and exact alignment (nonzero remainder ⇒ invalid), validates the block is allocated via region table `0xA0A6A5A0` (`0x7d9975de`), then releases it (`0x7d9975f2`); on a bad/unallocated pointer it prints `"scheap_large_free2: bad pointer :"` / `"not allocated pointer :"` (`0x7d998b36`/`0x7d998b58`) plus the pointer via `dbg_print_hex 0x7d999a53`. One member of the **scheap region-heap family** — sibling locked thunks `0x7d996fc6`→`scheap_middle_alloc`(`0x7d9971cc`, `"no more middle_heap"`), `0x7d996fe2`→`scheap_middle_free`, `0x7d996fd4`→`scheap_middle_free2`, `0x7d996ff0`→large-alloc — all built on `call_locked 0x7d996ffe`/`call_locked_arg 0x7d997031`. These `0xA0A5xxxx`-region heaps are the large image/work buffers (distinct from the on-chip `smallmem`/segregated `heap`). 143 xrefs | `symbol-map.md` |

## Capability / flag tests
| address | xrefs | symbol | role | source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0x7ddca1ce` | 1695 | `cap_test` | **`cap_test(mask=d0, group=d1)`** → `(*(0x60711d7c + group*4) & mask) != 0`. A 2-D capability matrix (group index × bit mask) in RAM table `0x60711d7c`. Sample sites: `(grp 4, mask 0x8000000)`, `(grp d2, mask 64)`. Candidate for the ROM capability family behind the ring-feature `descriptor_gate` | `symbol-map.md`, `community-dbfw-tool.md` |
| `0x7ddca28a` | — | `cap_group_of` | sibling that computes a capability `group` from an id (feeds `cap_test` as `d1`) | `symbol-map.md` |

RAM capability/flag table region: `0x60711xxx` (`0x60711d7c` matrix; neighbours `0x60711da8`,
`0x60711dac` read by the adjacent function) — inside the `0x60000000` RAM aperture.

## Object model — construction / dispatch / resolve
Corroborates the C++-style object model (vtable pointer at object `+0`; see `vcall_slot80`).
| address | xrefs | symbol | role | source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0x7e2053c1` | 2182 | `OMReactive_ctor` | constructor of the **OMReactive** refcounted base class: writes vtable `0x7e42c10c` to obj `+0`, stores arg at `+4` (refcount), clears `+8` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e2053d2` | 2029 | `OMReactive_ctor_noarg` | same class, no-arg variant (installs vtable, clears `+8`, returns null `a0`) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e206dc9` | 950 | `OMReactive_ref_dec` | refcount decrement at obj `+4`; on underflow loads msg `"underflow in OMREactive less then 0"` (`0x7e42c580`) and calls assert stub | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e206de4` | — | `OMReactive_ref_inc` | refcount increment at obj `+4`; on overflow (`0xffff`) loads `"overflow in OMREactive too many active"` (`0x7e42c5b0`) and calls assert stub | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e2053aa` | — | `assert_stub` | **release no-op** (`retf` only) — the error-report target for OMReactive refcount asserts does nothing in production firmware | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7dff548d` | 2639 | `log_fmt_msg` | **CORRECTED** (was `obj_factory_512`): formatted-log builder — alloc 512 B scratch (`0x7e206a17`), `sprintf` with format string `0x7e385ebc` (`"%1s..%1s, %d..[HLS]: %1s"`), `console_out 0x7d996e85`, then free. The "class tag" was a **format string**, not a vtable | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7da82515` | 2099 | `sprintf` | **CORRECTED** (was `ctor_thunk`): variadic `sprintf(dst_desc=a0, fmt=arg, …)` → calls the format core `0x7da811b7`. (`0x7da82530` is a *different* thunk → `0x7da8254b`, not a format wrapper — see Open) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9d36c4` | 2176 | `handle_to_obj` | resolve handle→object: if `a0==0` look up via `0x7d9d2bcc` (by id `d0`), else return `*(a0+4)` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9d2bcc` | — | `handle_table_lookup` | **id → object-descriptor table lookup** (the registry behind `handle_to_obj 0x7d9d36c4`). Table base `0xA0A4354C`, **8-byte entries**, live-count halfword at `0xa0a43548`; `entry = base + id*8`, returns `entry+4` (object ptr) when non-null. Zero count or null slot falls through to a second-table path (`id*8` reindex at `0x7d9d2bef`) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9dbe94` | — | `addr_region_classify` | **classify an address/value into a memory-region tag** by comparing against region bounds (`0x40000000`, `0x41000000`, `0x60000000`, `0x66000000`, …). Returns a small tag whose low 3 bits select access width in `reg_write_ctx` (tag `2` → 16-bit, `4` → 32-bit element). Used to pick the right store width for a handle's backing region (RAM vs SFR/shadow) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9d379b` | 228 | `reg_write_ctx` | **context-aware indexed typed write through a handle** (228 xrefs). Resolves the target object (handle `a0`, else id → `handle_table_lookup 0x7d9d2bcc`, taking `obj+4`), classifies the backing region via `addr_region_classify 0x7d9dbe94` and masks the tag to 3 bits to choose element width, then stores the value at byte-index `d3` — region tag `2` does `movhu` to **two** destinations `(d3,a2)` & `(d3,a0)` (live + shadow copy), tag `4` scales the index to word stride. Picks an **IRQ-safe path** by the active-interrupt SFR `*(0x8050)`: in-IRQ → `0x7d9d3483` (guards with `0x47001b0a` in-IRQ check + `irq_save 0x7d9c580c`), task context → worker `0x7d9d37c5`. Sibling entry `0x7d9d380e` = same with the value/index args read from a different slot. A peripheral/SFR-shadow register-write layer keyed by logical handle | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9d369a` | 126 | `handle_resolve_ctx` | **context-dispatched handle→object resolve** (READ-side sibling of `handle_to_obj 0x7d9d36c4`). `id = (u16)d0`; `obj = handle_table_lookup 0x7d9d2bcc(id)`; if null → return 0; else pass the descriptor's storage ptr `*(obj+4)` through the per-context dispatch core **`0x7d9d1cec`** and return its result (`a0`, used by callers as an object pointer, e.g. `0x7d9d21cf` then `cmp 0,a0`). `0x7d9d1cec` reads the active-context selector SFR `*(0x8050)` (the same IRQ/CPU selector `reg_write_ctx` keys on), indexes dispatch table `0x7d9df09c` by it, and `calls` that handler with the value — i.e. resolves/validates the object in the current (IRQ vs task) context. Paired sibling **`0x7d9d36b1`** = plain descriptor-field getter `return obj ? *(u16)(obj+2) : 0` (callers invoke both on the same id back-to-back: `+2` field + resolved object). **Dead end:** `0x7d9df09c` entries are not plain code pointers (SFR-indexed), so the exact per-context handler identity is unresolved | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e88256e` | 427 | `idx_record_set` | writes a **12-byte record** for a small id `a0` (must be `< 4`, else no-op) into array `0x60AD71DC`, physical slot via id→slot LUT `0x604522CC` (`slot = *(0x604522CC+id)`, offset `slot*12`). Stores caller payload `d0→+4` (byte), `d1→+6` (hw), plus two stacked args and RTOS context values fetched via `0x7e881fef`/`0x7e882003` (→ `0x7d9a1c58`/`0x7d9a1c14`, current task/tick). **Naming dead end**: no class/trace string; indirect LUT+record table, subsystem identity not recoverable. Looks like a per-channel ownership/profiling slot (≤4 channels) | `symbol-map.md` |

The **play-mode GUI picture-selection ("PicSel") wizard** state (multi-select flags, selection indices default 0xFFFF) | `symbol-map.md` |

Call sites pair the singleton getter then this (`call 0x7e3404e8; call 0x7e34055f; cmp <k>,d0`) and branch on small enum values (0, 1, …), so `+0` is the current picture-selection sub-mode. Companion **`clearPlayOrigin 0x7e34059f`** resets the struct (`+0=0`, `+4/+8/+12=0xFFFF`, zero `+16/+32/+36/+40/+42/+44`) and logs `"[GUI App]ClearPlayOrigin:PicSelFlag CLEAR"` (`0x7E5E6B11`) via `dbg_printf_lvl2 0x7e334de7`. On post **fail** (`d0==0`): if **+10043**==0 set error state **+8 = 6** and return 0, else return 1; on **success** set **+10042 = 1** (mark pending/in-flight). Pairs with drain `adj_wait_pending 0x7e72314a` and predicate `adj_block_ready 0x7e7230f2` (which instead dry-run-validates via `0x7e7230a7`→`0x7e7c79ab`). 94 call sites across the `0x7e72xxxx`–`0x7e72bxxx` adj engine gate a step on re-applying the block. Extends adj field map: **+10044 = u16 block id/length** (handed to the parser). **Dead end:** exact contents of the posted message / what the worker parses from the +9012 block not pinned (no local rodata in this module) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e722d5f` | 94 | `adj_commit_record` | **`adj_system` (`0x6091c53c`) — commit the current adjustment command + record it.** Thin entry (`clr d1; call worker 0x7e722d69`) with OK-flag arg = 0; `(d0 = state code)`. Worker **`0x7e722d69`**: latches the current register value **`+4 = *(+8368)`**; if `d0!=0` sets **command-state `+8 = d0`**; reloads `+8` and runs the dedup-append worker **`0x7e722bf2`** (same as `adj_dedup_record 0x7e722d55` — scans +10920 dedup array, emits/append a CSV record); on a nonzero dedup result tail-calls **`0x7e722817`** which stores the flag arg into **result/OK byte `+0`** (`0x7e722817` returns 1 iff `+0==1`). So this path commits the command with **`+0` cleared (0 = not-OK / plain commit)**, distinct from the diagnostic OK-path writer `adj_record_write 0x7e722da4` (which runs when `+8==1`). The worker's other entry is inline in `adj_record_write` (`0x7e722e4f`). 94 call sites pass a state code and commit. Extends adj field map: **+0 = result/OK flag (1 = command OK)**, **+4 = latched reg value (snapshot of +8368)**, **+8 = command-state code**. Cluster: `adj_dedup_record 0x7e722d55`, `adj_record_write 0x7e722da4`, `adj_set_reg8368 0x7e7229ac` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e722839` | 219 | `adj_get_flag12` | leaf getter `return (s8)*(adj_system+12)` (`adj_system` = `*(0x6091c53c)`). The init/parse path `0x7e7227d0` seeds **+12 = bit0 of adjustment-data key `0x57A4`** (read via `0x7e723660`→`0x7e7235a8`→`get_adj_system 0x7e722617`), so +12 is a **0/1 enable flag**; every call site tests it as boolean (`extb d0; cmp 0,d0; beq`). **219 xrefs** — an adj-feature gate. Sits in the adj_system header-accessor block: sibling getters `0x7e722846` → `(s8)+13`, multi-out `0x7e722853`(out `a0`←+4 u32, `a1`←+8 u16, `a3`←+20 u16, `d1`-ptr←+36 u16); setter `0x7e722895`(+4=d0, +8=d1). Extends adj field map: **+4 = u32 header, +8/+20/+36 = u16 header fields, +12 = enable flag (key 0x57A4 bit0)** | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e7229ac` | 518 | `adj_set_reg8368` | `adj_system` register setter (singleton `0x6091c53c`): stores arg `d0` → field **+8368**, clears status bytes **+8372** and **+8404**, then re-inits via `0x7e759152` and flushes via `0x7e722a88`. The flush (`0x7e722a88`) is gated on byte **+13** and, when set, formats/copies the **+9012** adjustment-data block (`%f`/`%d`, via `0x7e758c20`/`0x7da836ab`). Sibling `0x7e7229f5`/`a02` = set/get paired reg **+8364** | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e724078` | 1490 | `ctx_f10916_dispatch` | gets `adj_system` via `get_adj_system 0x7e722617`, gates on byte field `+10916`, then calls `0x7e723f81` on sub-struct `+10148` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e7240a9` | 752 | `ctx_f10916_getval` | sibling of `ctx_f10916_dispatch`: same ctx (`0x7e722617`), gate byte `+10916` + readiness `0x7e723f81` on sub-struct `+10148`; if ready & `*(substruct)!=0` returns 64-bit pair (`d0:d1`) from `0x7da8484e`, else passes through caller's `d0:d1` default. Neighbor `0x7e7240e2` clears `+10916` (disable). So `+10916`=feature enable flag, `+10148`=feature state struct | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e205040` | 1255 | `base_reset_vtbl_7e42c02c` | lifecycle reset/dtor stage: installs base vtable `0x7e42c02c` at obj `+0`, then `0x7e2050d6` zeroes member at obj `+24` if non-null; returns null `a0` | `symbol-map.md` |

Known vtable constants: `0x7e42c10c` (**OMReactive** refcounted base class, built by `OMReactive_ctor`), `0x7e42c02c` (base vtable installed by `base_reset_vtbl_7e42c02c`), `0x7e42c09c`
(sibling vtable installed nearby at `0x7e2050e2`). The `0x7e42cxxx` block is a **vtable array** for one
class hierarchy.

## OS / IPC primitives (hot, reused everywhere)
| address | symbol | role | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x7d9c132d` | `os_read` | generic blocking read/receive: `a0=handle obj (deref `(a0)`), d0=len/id, d1=timeout` (`-1`=block). **154 xrefs.** Dispatches to backend `0x7d9c4093` (timed) vs `0x7d9a7ba0` (blocking) after handle-validity check `0x7d9c3fa8`. Used by firmup (SD file read) + PC_PTP (queue drain) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9c1429` | `os_chan_io_prep` | channel I/O prep (bidirectional): seek/prep before `os_read` in firmup; also the **send** path in `console_out` (buf, len, handle, timeout `-1`) — not read-only | `camera-fw-update.md` |
| `0x7d9b0858` | `console_out_chan` | console output channel handle used by `console_out 0x7d996e85` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9c106e` | `OlyEventSet` | **OlyOS event-flag set** (187 xrefs): `(d0=event id, d1=bits)`. Picks ISR vs task kernel entry via context test `oly_in_isr 0x7d9c3fa8` → `0x7d9c410c` (ISR, label `"OlyEvStCpu"` `0x7D9EDD48`) or `0x7d9a682b` (task, `"OlyEvSt"` `0x7D9EDD53`); result via `oly_os_result_check 0x7d9c3e02`; error `"[OlEv]Err..EventSet ID :"` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9c1138` | `OlyEventWait` | **OlyOS event-flag wait** (191 xrefs): `(d0=id, d1=wait bits/mode, a0,a1=out, +40=timeout)`; `timeout==-1` short-circuits. ISR vs task via `oly_in_isr` → `0x7d9c417a` (`"OlyEvWtCpu"` `0x7D9EDDA1`) / task path; error `"[OlEv]Err..EventWait ID :"` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9c1606` | `OlySemaphoreWait` | **OlyOS semaphore acquire** (184 xrefs): `(d0=sem id, d1=timeout)`; `timeout==-1` short-circuits to the wait-forever path. ISR vs task context via `oly_in_isr 0x7d9c3fa8` → CPU-context entry `0x7d9c4275` (`"OlySmWtCpu"` `0x7D9EDEBC`) / task entry `0x7d9a8156` (`"OlySmWt"`), then the kernel wait primitive `0x7d9c3e10`. Return (in `a2`): **0 = acquired**, **-2 = timeout** (kernel `-50`), **-1 = error** → logs `"[OlSm]Err..SemaphoreWait ID :"` (`0x7D9EDECF`) with id + CPU ctx (SFR `0x8050`) via `0x7d9999f9`. Semaphore peer of `OlyEventWait` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9c16f9` | 173 | `OlySemaphoreSignal` | **OlyOS semaphore signal/post** (173 xrefs) — release counterpart to `OlySemaphoreWait 0x7d9c1606`. `(d0=sem id)`. ISR vs task context via `oly_in_isr 0x7d9c3fa8` → CPU-context entry `0x7d9c4241` (`"OlySmSiCpu"` `0x7D9EDF05`) / task entry `0x7d9a6935` (`"OlySmSi"` `0x7D9EDF10`), then error-check `0x7d9c3e02`. On failure (`result < 0`) logs `"[OlSm]Err..SemaphoreSignal ID :"` (`0x7D9EDF18`) with id + CPU ctx (SFR `0x8050`) + current task id (`0x7d9a1c14`) via the OlyOS error formatter `0x7d9999f9` (also `"[OlSm]tID :"` `0x7D9EDEED` / `"[OlSm]CPU :"` `0x7D9EDEF9`). Returns result code in `d0` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9c3fa8` | `oly_in_isr` | OlyOS **execution-context test**: calls RAM kernel routine `0x47001b0a`; `==1` → in-ISR/non-task (logs, returns 0) so callers select the `*Cpu` kernel entry. Shared by `OlyEventSet/Wait`, `OlySemaphoreWait`, `os_read`, and peers | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d9c3e02` | `oly_os_result_check` | OlyOS call-result check: `d2<0` (error) → error-logger `0x7d9c3e26`; returns the code. Sibling `0x7d9c3e10` also whitelists `-50`/`-2` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7D9EDC80`–`0x7D9EDF00` | `olyos_label_pool` | OlyOS wrapper label/error-string pool: **`[OlEv]`** event (`OlyEvSt/Wt/Cl/Dl` + `*Cpu`/`i*` variants, `EventSet/Wait/Clear/Delete ID :`), **`[OlQe]`** queue (`OlyQeRd/Wr/Dl`, `QueueRead/Write/Delete`), **`[OlSm]`** semaphore (`OlySmWt/Dl`, `SemaphoreWait/Delete`). Confirms a full `OlyEv`/`OlyQe`/`OlySm` RTOS-abstraction API over the µITRON kernel | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7dfc0587` | `lock_get` | get lock object for id `d0`; lazy-inits the lock-manager singleton at `0x6044cf20` (alloc 44 B via `0x7e206a17` if null) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7dfc056a` | `mutex_lock` | virtual `lock()`: inner obj `*(a0+4)`, `vtbl=*(obj)`, `calls *(vtbl+12)` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7dfc057a` | `mutex_unlock` | virtual `unlock()`: inner obj `*(a0+4)`, `vtbl=*(obj)`, `calls *(vtbl+16)` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e02ec5a` | `get_gui_app` | **double-checked-locking** getter for the **GUI App settings** singleton `0x6044eb38` (1061 xrefs): if `*(0x6044eb38)` null, take lock id 2 (`lock_get`+`mutex_lock`), re-check, alloc 8 B + ctor `0x7e02ecb2`, store, release. Ctor `0x7e02ecb2`: obj+0 = **vtable `0x7E3A472C`** (code ptrs `0x7e02f…`; slot+0=`setSetting 0x7e02f92b`, slot+4=`getSetting 0x7e02f03c`), obj+4=0; then `0x7e05371c`→`0x7e02fd97` finish init. **CORRECTION:** earlier notes transposed the hex and called obj+0 a "static config record `0x7e473a2c` {15,15,…}" — that is a different, unrelated data address; obj+0 is the vtable `0x7E3A472C` | `symbol-map.md` |

## libc-style primitives
| address | symbol | role | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x7da837b0` | `strncmp` | bounded compare: `a0=s1, a1=s2, d0=n`; returns 0 on equal-within-n or NUL, else signed byte diff. Used by `usr_checksum` id==2 path + region id-check | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7da837d4` | `strncpy` | bounded copy `a1→a0` up to `d0`, stops at NUL then pads (immediately follows `strncmp`) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7da83862` | `strstr` | **strstr** `(haystack=a0, needle=a1)` — returns pointer to first occurrence of `needle` in `haystack`, or `NULL` if absent; empty `needle` returns `haystack`, empty `haystack` with non-empty needle returns `NULL`. Outer loop finds a matching first char, inner `setlb`/`lra` HW-loop compares the rest; advances `a0` on mismatch. 103 xrefs. App-cluster `0x7da83xxx` libc (same bank as `strcmp 0x7da836f1`/`strlen 0x7da8376d`/`strncmp 0x7da837b0`); immediately precedes the `setjmp 0x7da8389e`/`longjmp 0x7da838b8` pair | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d997713` | `strcpy` | **strcpy** `(dst=a0, src=a1)` — byte-copy to NUL (`movbu`/`setlb`/`lne` loop), writes terminating NUL, returns the NUL ptr in `dst` (`a2`). 150 xrefs. Core `0x7d99xxxx` libc copy, sibling of `strcat 0x7d99772d`, `strncmp 0x7d997751`, `strcmp 0x7d997773`, `strlen 0x7d99779f`; distinct from app-cluster `strcpy 0x7da83710` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d99772d` | `strcat` | **strcat** `(dst=a0, src=a1)` — finds `strlen(dst)` via `0x7d99779f`, appends `src` bytes after it + NUL, returns `dst` (`a0`). Core `0x7d99xxxx` copy; cf. app-cluster `strcat 0x7da836ab` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d997751` | `strncmp` | **strncmp** `(a0, a1, n=d0)` — bounded byte compare, returns 0 when `n` bytes equal or both strings end, else signed `*a0−*a1` at first mismatch. Core `0x7d99xxxx` copy, sibling of `strcmp 0x7d997773` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d997773` | `strcmp` | unbounded byte compare `a0` vs `a1`; returns signed `*a0-*a1` at first mismatch, 0 if equal (574 xrefs). **Separate copy** in the core `0x7d99xxxx` region — distinct from the `0x7da83xxx` string cluster (two libc instances linked) | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7d99779f` | `strlen` | scan-to-NUL in core region (sibling of `strcmp 0x7d997773`); distinct from `strlen 0x7da8376d` | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x7e6e23f9` | `memcpy_thunk` | **memcpy thunk** (118 xrefs) — bare tail-call forwarding `(a0=dst, a1=src, d0=n)` to ROM **`memcpy 0x47001000`** (the mask-ROM libc at `0x47000000`). A per-module libc shim (one of a small thunk bank): neighbors **`0x7e6e241f` = memset thunk** (→ ROM `0x4700110c`), **`0x7e6e242a` = strlen** (inline `movbu`/`setlb`/`lne` scan, returns `a1−a0`; third copy alongside `strlen 0x7d99779f`/`0x7da8376d`). 118 call sites in this module reach memcpy through this local entry rather than the ROM address directly | `symbol-map.md` |
| `0x47001000` | `memcpy` | mask-ROM `memcpy(dst=a0, src=a1, len=d0)` — 1394 xrefs | `memory-map.md` |
| `0x4700110c` | `memset` | mask-ROM `memset(dst=a0, val=d0, len=d1)` — 3612 xrefs (was mislabeled byte-copy) | `memory-map.md` |
